❯ # FINN Phase 12A — Reliable Automation Foundation + Scheduled Market Data

  Continue from the current FINN repository. First read:

  - `docs/FINN_PRODUCT_GAP_ANALYSIS.md`
  - `docs/FINN_COMPETITIVE_BENCHMARK.md`
  - `docs/FINN_IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP.md`

  Also inspect the existing backend, market-data services, database schema,
  deployment configuration and tests.

  ## Objective

  Build a reliable foundation for background automation and implement the first
  complete automated workflow: **scheduled EOD market-data refresh for the
  frozen 20-company portfolio**.

  This is a focused implementation phase. Do not attempt every automation
  feature at once.

  ## 1. Audit before coding

  Determine:

  - Whether a scheduler or background worker already exists.
  - How the application is deployed and whether scheduled execution survives
  restarts.
  - How market data is currently fetched, stored and exposed through the API.
  - Whether refreshes are currently triggered manually or automatically.
  - How errors, stale data and duplicate executions are handled.

  Reuse existing components wherever practical. Record the findings in
  `docs/FINN_AUTOMATION_FOUNDATION.md`.

  ## 2. Build reliable job execution

  Implement a reusable job-execution mechanism suitable for future document
  discovery, announcement monitoring, ingestion and alert evaluation.

  It should support:

  - Unique job/run IDs.
  - Job status: pending, running, completed, failed.
  - Start and completion timestamps.
  - Useful execution logs and error messages.
  - Retry with bounded exponential backoff.
  - Timeouts.
  - Duplicate-run protection.
  - Safe recovery after application or worker restart.
  - Concurrency limits.
  - Idempotent execution.

  Do not rely on an in-memory scheduler as the only mechanism for production
  execution.

  Inspect the actual deployment configuration before deciding how jobs should
  be triggered. Prefer a durable worker or externally triggered scheduled
  command where appropriate. Do not silently add a paid service or change
  deployment infrastructure.

  If a database table is required, use a safe additive migration. Preserve all
  existing data.

  ## 3. Automate EOD market-data refresh

  Use the existing yfinance integration.

  The refresh should:

  1. Read portfolio tickers from the existing `PortfolioRepository`.
  2. Fetch the latest available EOD data.
  3. Validate the returned data.
  4. Store or update it safely.
  5. Record the data's actual market date.
  6. Track the last successful refresh.
  7. Record failures by company.
  8. Retry transient errors without duplicating records.

  Use all 20 frozen portfolio companies. Do not hardcode them into the new job.

  Keep the five ad-hoc companies separate from portfolio Push.

  ### Data-quality requirements

  - Clearly label data as EOD, never real-time.
  - Do not treat weekends or market holidays as missing-data failures.
  - Do not overwrite good historical data with invalid or empty responses.
  - Do not report a company refresh as successful when its data was not
  validated.
  - Track freshness separately from job execution time.
  - Handle partial failures: one company failing must not stop valid updates
  for the other 19.

  ## 4. Scheduling

  Schedule the refresh after the Indian market closes, allowing time for EOD
  data to become available.

  Use the appropriate Asia/Kolkata timezone.

  Make the schedule configurable through environment variables or existing
  application settings.

  Also provide a manual command or endpoint for an authorised operator to
  trigger a refresh.

  Prevent simultaneous scheduled and manual executions from processing the same
  refresh twice.

  Do not expose an unrestricted job-trigger endpoint publicly.

  ## 5. Operational visibility

  Add a simple way for an authorised operator to inspect:

  - Latest run status.
  - Last successful run.
  - Latest market-data date.
  - Companies refreshed successfully.
  - Companies that failed.
  - Current data freshness.
  - Error details.
  - Next scheduled execution, if determinable.

  Keep internal technical diagnostics out of the normal investor-facing Home
  screen.

  A compact status page or existing admin endpoint is sufficient. Do not
  undertake a major UI redesign in this phase.

  ## 6. Tests

  Add tests for:

  - Successful refresh.
  - Partial company failures.
  - Empty and malformed market-data responses.
  - Duplicate-run prevention.
  - Retry behaviour.
  - Worker restart and recovery.
  - Weekend and holiday handling.
  - Timezone correctness.
  - Historical data preservation.
  - Correct use of the live portfolio repository.
  - Separation of ad-hoc research from portfolio updates.

  Run the full existing test suite and preserve the previous regression
  baseline.

  Do not use live external calls in ordinary unit tests. Mock the market-data
  provider and add a separate controlled integration test where appropriate.

  ## 7. Acceptance criteria

  This phase is complete only when:

  - A scheduled refresh can execute without a user manually opening the
  application.
  - All 20 portfolio companies are considered.
  - Valid updates are stored safely.
  - Partial failures are visible and recoverable.
  - Duplicate executions are prevented.
  - Data freshness is accurate.
  - All data is labelled EOD.
  - Existing Push, Pull, portfolio and document functionality remains intact.
  - Existing regression tests pass.
  - The execution method is compatible with the actual deployment architecture.

  ## 8. Do not do in this phase

  - Do not change the 20-company portfolio.
  - Do not change the five ad-hoc companies.
  - Do not reprocess or delete existing documents or chunks.
  - Do not introduce paid market-data providers.
  - Do not add unofficial NSE/BSE scraping.
  - Do not rewrite Push or Pull.
  - Do not undertake a broad frontend redesign.
  - Do not claim the scheduler works in production unless the deployment path
  has actually been verified.

  ## 9. Final report

  Create `docs/FINN_PHASE_12A_REPORT.md` containing:

  - What was implemented.
  - Architecture and scheduling method.
  - How to run and configure it.
  - Test results.
  - Actual deployment requirements.
  - Known limitations.
  - Any manual setup still required.
  - Recommended next implementation phase.

  Implement the work, run the tests and report honestly. Do not stop at a
  design proposal.

⏺ Phase 12A is implemented, tested, and documented. Summary:

  Built:
  - A reusable job-execution foundation (JobRunRepository + run_with_retry) —
    durable run tracking, duplicate-run prevention, crash recovery, bounded
    retry/timeout — designed for reuse by future jobs, not just this one.
  - The first automated workflow: scheduled EOD market-data refresh
    (run_market_data_refresh()), reading tickers from the live
    PortfolioRepository (never hardcoded), reusing the existing
    market_data.fetch_history unmodified, storing only validated closed EOD bars
    idempotently into the previously-dormant prices table.
  - Admin endpoints (POST /api/admin/market-data/refresh, GET
    /api/admin/market-data/status), token-gated, 503 when unconfigured, 409 on
    duplicate runs.
  - Two scheduling paths: an optional in-process APScheduler (off by default)
    and a standalone CLI script for external cron — documented honestly as to
    which is actually production-durable.
  - Additive migration 010 widening the existing (unused) ingestion_jobs table.

  Tested: 18 new tests (successful refresh, partial failure, malformed/empty
  data, duplicate-run prevention, retry, crash recovery, weekend/holiday
  handling, timezone correctness, historical-data preservation, admin auth/409)
  — all mocked, no live calls. Full suite: 487 passed, 0 regressions (up from
  the 469 baseline).

  Honest limitations flagged in docs/FINN_PHASE_12A_REPORT.md: migration 010 is
  written but not applied (consistent with this repo's own practice of treating
  migrations as human-applied proposals — the Supabase REST client can't run DDL
  anyway); FINN isn't actually deployed anywhere yet, so no production cron was
  configured or claimed to work; the live quotes endpoint still fetches from
  yfinance directly rather than reading the newly-stored history (flagged as the
  natural next phase, not bundled in to keep this phase's blast radius
  contained).

  Three docs written: docs/FINN_AUTOMATION_FOUNDATION.md (pre-implementation
  audit), and docs/FINN_PHASE_12A_REPORT.md (final report with manual setup
  steps). The 20-company portfolio, ad-hoc set, and existing documents/chunks
  were untouched.

❯ # FINN Phase 12B — Automatic Company Document Discovery and Ingestion

  Continue from the completed Phase 12A implementation.

  Read these documents first:

  - `docs/FINN_PRODUCT_GAP_ANALYSIS.md`
  - `docs/FINN_IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP.md`
  - `docs/FINN_AUTOMATION_FOUNDATION.md`
  - `docs/FINN_PHASE_12A_REPORT.md`

  Inspect the existing document repository, PDF acquisition and extraction
  pipeline, company registry, Supabase schema, `JobRunRepository`,
  `run_with_retry()` and current deployment configuration.

  ## Objective

  Make FINN automatically discover, acquire, process and index new company
  financial documents.

  The investor should not need to find and upload ordinary company reports
  manually.

  Prioritise reliable automatic coverage over pretending that every source is
  accessible.

  ## 1. Audit document coverage

  Build an accurate inventory for all 20 portfolio companies.

  For each company, identify:

  - Annual reports available.
  - Quarterly results available.
  - Investor presentations available.
  - Earnings-call transcripts available.
  - Latest reporting period for each document type.
  - Existing documents already stored in FINN.
  - Latest successful ingestion date.
  - Official investor-relations website.
  - Available document-source URLs.
  - Source accessibility and restrictions.

  Use the live repository and database state where available. Do not rely on
  old reports if the current state can be checked.

  Preserve the five ad-hoc companies as independent research entities. Their
  document discovery must not automatically add them to portfolio Push.

  Create `docs/FINN_DOCUMENT_COVERAGE.md`.

  ## 2. Build reusable document-source adapters

  Prioritise sources in this order:

  1. Official company investor-relations websites.
  2. Official company annual-report and results pages.
  3. Accessible official company-issued document repositories.
  4. Screener.in links where access is permitted.
  5. Other legitimate sources that link to the original company-issued
  document.

  Do not assume that NSE/BSE automated access is available. Do not bypass bot
  protection, authentication, access restrictions or licensing controls.

  Build reusable source adapters instead of hardcoding individual download
  scripts.

  Each adapter should:

  - Discover candidate document links.
  - Identify PDF documents.
  - Extract available metadata.
  - Resolve relative URLs.
  - Validate source identity.
  - Handle redirects and timeouts safely.
  - Respect reasonable request limits.
  - Record source failures.
  - Support incremental discovery.

  Avoid unnecessary browser automation or large scraping dependencies.

  ## 3. Discover the correct documents

  Support these document types:

  - Annual reports.
  - Quarterly and annual financial results.
  - Investor presentations.
  - Earnings-call transcripts.
  - Other relevant company-issued financial documents.

  For each candidate, identify:

  - Company identity.
  - Document type.
  - Document title.
  - Reporting period.
  - Publication date.
  - Source URL.
  - Original filename.
  - Discovery timestamp.

  A document must not be assigned to a company solely because its URL contains
  a ticker.

  Validate company name and document context.

  Do not confuse TMPV and TMCV. Preserve the existing company-resolution rules.

  If the source identity is uncertain, send the candidate to a review queue
  instead of ingesting it.

  ## 4. Incremental discovery and deduplication

  Discovery must be incremental.

  Before downloading or ingesting:

  - Check the canonical source URL.
  - Check document hash where available.
  - Check existing document metadata.
  - Check company identity.
  - Check reporting period.
  - Check existing document IDs.

  Do not reprocess existing documents simply because they appear again on a
  website.

  Prioritise coverage gaps first.

  For initial backfill, use configurable lookback limits and document-count
  limits per company. Prioritise recent quarterly results and transcripts
  needed for four-quarter comparisons, along with recent annual reports.

  Do not indiscriminately download entire document archives.

  ## 5. Safe automatic ingestion

  Integrate with the existing PDF ingestion pipeline.

  For each high-confidence document:

  1. Download the PDF.
  2. Validate file type and file integrity.
  3. Verify company identity.
  4. Determine document type and reporting period.
  5. Compute SHA256.
  6. Check for duplicates.
  7. Create or resolve document metadata.
  8. Run existing extraction and chunking.
  9. Validate extraction outcome.
  10. Store chunks and metadata.
  11. Make the document available to Pull.
  12. Record the final processing status.

  Reuse the existing extraction and positional-extraction logic.

  Do not rewrite the ingestion pipeline unless a verified problem requires it.

  ### Important integrity rules

  - Never delete existing chunks.
  - Never overwrite a successfully processed document with a failed extraction.
  - Never silently change a document's company association.
  - Never reuse another company's document.
  - Do not treat a downloaded but invalid PDF as successfully ingested.
  - Do not mark low-confidence metadata as verified.
  - Preserve the existing numeric fact and period-integrity protections.

  Only automatically ingest documents that pass the necessary checks.

  Ambiguous documents must go into a review queue.

  ## 6. Reuse Phase 12A job execution

  Implement document discovery as a scheduled job using the existing:

  - `JobRunRepository`
  - `run_with_retry()`
  - Duplicate-run protection.
  - Failure handling.
  - Execution history.
  - Recovery behaviour.

  Do not create a second scheduler or separate job framework.

  If Phase 12A's migration 010 has not been applied, detect that condition and
  report the exact setup requirement.

  Do not assume the database schema exists merely because a migration file
  exists.

  If additional persistent tables are genuinely required, create additive
  migration proposals and document the manual application process. Do not apply
  migrations or make destructive database changes.

  ## 7. Persist source tracking

  Track source-level progress for every company.

  At minimum, preserve:

  - Last checked timestamp.
  - Last successful check.
  - Last discovered document.
  - Latest processed document.
  - Source URL.
  - Source health.
  - Error status.
  - Next eligible check, where applicable.

  Use a durable store.

  Do not rely on in-memory Python dictionaries for production checkpoints.

  If an official source becomes inaccessible, mark it as blocked or degraded
  and retain the last known successful state.

  ## 8. Schedule and run

  Provide:

  - A scheduled incremental discovery job.
  - An authorised manual discovery trigger.
  - A status endpoint or admin view.
  - Per-company execution results.
  - Failed-document visibility.
  - A review queue for uncertain candidates.

  Reuse Phase 12A's scheduling model and token-gated admin conventions.

  Do not expose unrestricted public download or ingestion endpoints.

  Do not claim production scheduling is active unless the deployment
  configuration actually supports it.

  ## 9. Improve research readiness

  Update existing research-readiness information using the newly discovered and
  ingested documents.

  The investor should be able to understand:

  - Whether annual reports are available.
  - Whether recent quarterly results are available.
  - Whether transcripts are available.
  - How many reporting periods are covered.
  - When the company data was last updated.
  - What remains unavailable.

  Do not inflate coverage just because a URL was discovered. Distinguish
  discovered, downloaded, processed and searchable documents.

  ## 10. Tests

  Add mocked tests for:

  - Official source discovery.
  - Multiple document types.
  - Incorrect company identity.
  - TMPV/TMCV separation.
  - Duplicate URLs.
  - Duplicate document hashes.
  - Duplicate reporting periods.
  - Invalid PDFs.
  - Failed downloads.
  - Partial company failures.
  - Source timeouts.
  - Retry handling.
  - Incremental checkpoints.
  - Existing document preservation.
  - Crash recovery.
  - Manual-review routing.
  - Successful ingestion into the existing pipeline.
  - Research-readiness updates.

  Run the full existing regression suite. The previous baseline was 487 passing
  tests.

  Do not use uncontrolled live scraping in ordinary tests.

  ## 11. Acceptance criteria

  This phase is complete only when:

  - FINN can discover new documents without manual uploading.
  - Discovery is reusable across companies.
  - At least one accessible official source completes an end-to-end
  discovery-to-searchable-document workflow.
  - Existing documents and chunks remain unchanged.
  - Duplicate documents are not processed again.
  - Source failures are recorded honestly.
  - Low-confidence candidates are reviewed rather than blindly ingested.
  - Portfolio membership is unchanged.
  - Ad-hoc companies remain independent of portfolio Push.
  - Job execution uses the Phase 12A foundation.
  - Tests pass.

  Do not mark all 20 companies as automatically covered merely because an
  adapter exists. Report actual results by company and source.

  ## 12. Documentation and final report

  Create:

  - `docs/FINN_PHASE_12B_REPORT.md`
  - `docs/FINN_DOCUMENT_SOURCE_REGISTRY.md`

  Report:

  - Sources implemented.
  - Companies successfully checked.
  - Documents discovered.
  - Documents downloaded.
  - Documents successfully ingested.
  - Documents rejected or sent for review.
  - Companies still blocked.
  - Exact reasons for blocked sources.
  - Tests and results.
  - Migration requirements.
  - Deployment setup.
  - Remaining manual work.

  **Final principle:** FINN should automatically collect and process
  trustworthy primary documents whenever permitted. When a source is
  inaccessible or a document is ambiguous, it must explain the problem rather
  than fabricate coverage.

  Implement this phase, run the complete regression suite and report actual
  end-to-end results.

⏺ Phase 12B is implemented, tested, and documented. Summary:

  Built: two reusable, generalizable document-source adapters
  (ScreenerSourceAdapter — verified live against all 20 portfolio companies —
  and IRWebsiteSourceAdapter — generic crawler, one verified seed URL); a
  discovery job (run_document_discovery()) that reuses Phase 12A's
  JobRunRepository/retry/crash-recovery foundation rather than building a second
  framework; dedup/triage logic (by URL and by doc_type+period) before any
  download; a review queue for unverified candidates; durable per-source health
  tracking; admin endpoints and scheduling matching Phase 12A's conventions
  exactly; migration 011 (proposed, not applied, per established practice).

  Real, live proof (not simulated): discovered, downloaded, and ingested a
  genuine new document for TCS (which had zero prior coverage) through the
  unmodified existing ingestion pipeline against the live production database —
  74 chunks, confirmed Pull-searchable, and check_research_readiness("TCS")
  flipped to "ready" automatically with zero code changes.

  A real bug was caught and fixed mid-phase: the identity-matching heuristic
  (60% token overlap) would have wrongly confused "Tata Motors Passenger
  Vehicles" with a hypothetical "Tata Motors Commercial Vehicles" — caught by a
  test modeling exactly the TMPV/TMCV risk the brief warned about, fixed to
  require full token containment, re-verified against real page titles.

  Tests: 21 new (8 adapter, 13 job-orchestration), all mocked. Full suite: 508
  passed, 0 regressions (up from 487). One genuine test-pollution bug (an
  unmocked live call perturbing an unrelated embedding-ranking test via timing)
  was found, reproduced deliberately to confirm root cause, and fixed.

  Honest gaps, not papered over: only TCS was actually backfilled live; 6 more
  zero-coverage portfolio companies are confirmed discoverable but not yet
  ingested; migration 011 needs manual application; no production scheduler is
  active since FINN isn't deployed anywhere. All documented in
  docs/FINN_DOCUMENT_COVERAGE.md, docs/FINN_DOCUMENT_SOURCE_REGISTRY.md, and
  docs/FINN_PHASE_12B_REPORT.md.

❯ # FINN Phase 12C — Complete Push, Pull, UI and Deployment Readiness

  You are continuing development of FINN, an AI-powered financial investment
  research assistant.

  Phases 12A and 12B are complete. Your task is to implement the remaining
  high-value requirements from the original ENAM AI Engineer Build Challenge
  and bring FINN to a polished, reliable, demo-ready state.

  **Do not start another broad rewrite. Complete the product using the existing
  architecture.**

  The central product principle:

  FINN should automatically collect information, process it, identify important
  developments and explain them in simple language. The investor should mainly
  review important updates, ask questions and make decisions.

  The investor should not have to operate the data pipeline.

  ---

  # PART 1 — Read and understand the project

  Before changing anything, read:

  - `docs/FINN_PRODUCT_GAP_ANALYSIS.md`
  - `docs/FINN_COMPETITIVE_BENCHMARK.md`
  - `docs/FINN_IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP.md`
  - `docs/FINN_AUTOMATION_FOUNDATION.md`
  - `docs/FINN_PHASE_12A_REPORT.md`
  - `docs/FINN_PHASE_12B_REPORT.md`
  - `docs/FINN_DOCUMENT_COVERAGE.md`
  - `docs/FINN_DOCUMENT_SOURCE_REGISTRY.md`

  Also read the original ENAM challenge PDFs if available.

  Inspect the current codebase and deployed application.

  Understand:

  - FastAPI backend.
  - Frontend architecture and routing.
  - Supabase schema and repositories.
  - Job execution and retry framework.
  - Document discovery and ingestion.
  - PDF extraction and financial-fact extraction.
  - Pull research and source citations.
  - Push digest and event services.
  - Market-data refresh.
  - Existing admin endpoints.
  - Deployment and environment configuration.
  - Current test suite.

  Verify the actual implementation rather than trusting previous reports.

  ## Existing verified foundation

  Phase 12A:

  - Reusable `JobRunRepository` and `run_with_retry()`.
  - Durable job tracking and duplicate-run prevention.
  - Crash recovery and bounded retries.
  - Scheduled EOD market-data refresh.
  - Token-gated admin endpoints.
  - Migration 010 proposed but not applied.
  - No production scheduler configured yet.

  Phase 12B:

  - `ScreenerSourceAdapter`.
  - `IRWebsiteSourceAdapter`.
  - `run_document_discovery()`.
  - Reuse of Phase 12A's job execution framework.
  - URL and document-period deduplication.
  - Manual-review queue.
  - Durable source-health tracking.
  - Migration 011 proposed but not applied.
  - TCS document successfully discovered, downloaded and ingested live, with 74
  searchable chunks.
  - Six other z

  ompany identities.

  8. Do not make destructive Supabase changes.

  # PART 12 — Final deliverables

  Create:

  - `docs/FINN_PHASE_12C_REPORT.md`

  - `docs/FINN_DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md`

  The final product review must state:

  1. What works end-to-end.
  2. What works only in local development.
  3. What requires manual migration.
  4. What requires deployment configuration.
  5. Which companies have actual document coverage.
  6. Which announcement sources were verified.
  7. Which features remain incomplete.
  8. Full test results.
  9. Known bugs.
  10. Remaining risks.

  Provide a short demo walkthrough that can be completed in approximately three
  minutes.

  ## Final definition of done

  FINN is ready when:

  - It automatically refreshes EOD market data.
  - It automatically discovers and ingests documents from accessible sources.
  - It monitors accessible announcements.
  - It identifies and prioritises important portfolio developments.
  - It generates a useful daily briefing.
  - It supports configurable EOD alerts.
  - It answers research questions using primary company evidence.
  - It can compare reported results and management guidance where sufficient
  data exists.
  - It has a simple, polished mobile-first interface.
  - The original company universe and existing documents remain intact.
  - The full regression suite passes.
  - Deployment requirements are clearly documented.

  **Do not stop after writing a plan. Implement the highest-priority features,
  test them, fix failures and continue until the P0 workflows are working.**

  The final product should feel like a quiet investment research analyst
  working in the background: collecting information, identifying what matters,
  explaining it clearly and showing the evidence.

  The goal is a finished, convincing FINN prototype — not an endless
  engineering project.

❯ # FINN — Complete UI/UX Redesign and Investment Intelligence Upgrade

  Act as a senior product designer, frontend architect and financial research
  product engineer.

  We are upgrading FINN, an AI-powered financial investment research
  application.

  The goal is to make FINN visually attractive, mobile-first, intelligent and
  exceptionally easy to use.

  This is not just a CSS redesign. The application already retrieves financial
  information, market data, announcements and company documents. The problem is
  that the information is not always presented in a way that helps an investor
  understand what happened, why it matters and what to do next.

  Your job is to improve the actual product experience.

  **Three core objectives:**

  1. Make Portfolio and Push visually intelligent, with important developments
  immediately visible.
  2. Make Research turn retrieved information into clear, evidence-backed
  insights and next steps.
  3. Make the entire application beautiful, responsive and easy to use on
  mobile and desktop.

  Implement these changes in one coordinated development effort. Inspect the
  existing project, reuse working functionality, make the improvements, test
  the result and document what changed.

  ---

  # PART 1 — Inspect the current application

  Before modifying anything, inspect:

  - Frontend framework and project structure.
  - Existing routes and navigation.
  - Portfolio page and company components.
  - Daily Intelligence / Home page.
  - Research page.
  - Alerts page.
  - API integrations.
  - Existing chart libraries.
  - Existing market-data APIs.
  - Event and announcement data structures.
  - Financial research response structures.
  - Existing theme and CSS setup.
  - Responsive breakpoints.
  - Loading, empty and error states.

  Read the latest project documentation, especially:

  - `docs/FINN_PRODUCT_GAP_ANALYSIS.md`
  - `docs/FINN_COMPETITIVE_BENCHMARK.md`
  - `docs/FINN_PHASE_12C_REPORT.md`, if it exists.
  - Other recent phase reports.

  Do not assume every feature mentioned in the documentation is implemented.
  Check the code and actual API responses.

  Use the screenshots supplied with this request as references for the current
  UI and its problems.

  Do not rebuild the application from scratch.

  Preserve existing working backend functionality, API contracts, database
  structure and company identities.

  ---

  # PART 2 — Portfolio page: redesign completely

  This is one of the most important screens.

  The existing desktop table is usable but not sufficiently informative. On
  mobile, the table b
  ──── (865 lines hidden) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  CSS compilation.

  ---

  # PART 11 — Implementation method

  Work in this order:

  **Step 1 — Audit**
  Identify the existing components, API contracts and root causes of the
  issues.

  **Step 2 — Design system**
  Set up reusable theme tokens, typography, responsive layouts and shared
  components.

  **Step 3 — Portfolio**
  Fix the 15-versus-20 discrepancy, add search, sector grouping, responsive
  company cards and genuine sparklines.

  **Step 4 — Research**
  Implement the insight-first response layout, clear company assessment,
  evidence citations, financial visualisations and next actions.

  **Step 5 — Alerts**
  Fix event labels, priority semantics, trigger explanations and watch-rule
  presentation.

  **Step 6 — Home**
  Improve the daily investor briefing and its visual hierarchy.

  **Step 7 — Integration and testing**
  Verify every route, theme, mobile layout and data interaction.

  Do not stop after producing a design proposal. Implement the improvements.

  ---

  # PART 12 — Final deliverables

  Create:

  `docs/FINN_UI_REDESIGN_REPORT.md`

  Include:

  - Design changes.
  - Portfolio count root cause and fix.
  - New responsive behaviour.
  - Theme implementation.
  - Research insight improvements.
  - Alert labelling improvements.
  - APIs changed, if any.
  - Tests performed.
  - Before-and-after observations.
  - Known limitations.

  Provide actual desktop and mobile screenshots, if browser testing is
  available.

  ## Final definition of done

  FINN must feel like one coherent professional product.

  A user should be able to:

  1. Open Portfolio and immediately see all tracked companies.
  2. Understand price direction, change and volume without opening every
  company.
  3. Search for a company immediately.
  4. Scan sector groups and identify positive developments or companies needing
  attention.
  5. Switch between light and dark themes.
  6. Ask FINN a research question and receive a clear conclusion, reasons and
  supporting evidence.
  7. Understand whether the evidence supports a favourable, mixed or cautious
  company view.
  8. See what should be investigated next.
  9. Open Alerts and immediately understand what happened and why it triggered.

  **The most important principle: FINN must stop behaving like a collection of
  financial data tables and start behaving like an intelligent investment
  assistant.**

  Make information easier to understand, decisions easier to investigate and
  the overall application more enjoyable to use.

  Complete the implementation without damaging the verified backend, portfolio
  or document data.

      130      return build_price_quote(ticker, yfinance_symbol)

      132  
      133 +def get_price_history(ticker: str, yfinance_symbol: str, days:
          + int = 30, hist: pd.DataFrame | None = None) -> PriceHistoryRe
          +sponse:                                                       
      134 +    """CLOSED EOD daily bars for a sparkline/small chart — the
          + last `days`                                                  
      135 +    closed trading sessions, oldest first. Reuses fetch_histor
          +y (the exact                                                  
      136 +    same real yfinance call build_price_quote already uses) ra
          +ther than a                                                   
      137 +    second data path; never fabricates a bar for a day with no
          + real data                                                    
      138 +    (a provisional/intraday "today" row is excluded the same w
          +ay                                                            
      139 +    build_price_quote excludes it from its own averages)."""  
      140 +    if hist is None:                                          
      141 +        hist = fetch_history(yfinance_symbol, period="3mo")   
      142 +                                                              
      143 +    if hist is None or hist.empty:                            
      144 +        return PriceHistoryResponse(ticker=ticker, data_availa
          +ble=False, error="No data returned by yfinance for this symbol
          +.")                                                           
      145 +                                                              
      146 +    hist = hist.sort_index()                                  
      147 +    today = _today_ist()                                      
      148 +    is_today = hist.index.tz_convert(IST).normalize() == today
      149 +    closed = hist.loc[~is_today]                              
      150 +                                                              
      151 +    if closed.empty:                                          
      152 +        return PriceHistoryResponse(ticker=ticker, data_availa
          +ble=False, error="No closed EOD bars available yet.")         
      153 +                                                              
      154 +    window = closed.tail(max(days, 1))                        
      155 +    bars = [                                                  
      156 +        DailyBar(                                             
      157 +            trade_date=idx.tz_convert(IST).date(), open=round(
          +float(row["Open"]), 4), high=round(float(row["High"]), 4),    
      158 +            low=round(float(row["Low"]), 4), close=round(float
          +(row["Close"]), 4), volume=int(row["Volume"]),                
      159 +            session_status=SessionStatus.CLOSED_EOD,          
      160 +        )                                                     
      161 +        for idx, row in window.iterrows()                     
      162 +    ]                                                         
      163 +    return PriceHistoryResponse(ticker=ticker, data_available=
          +True, bars=bars)                                              
      164 +                                                              
      165 +                                                              
      166  CALCULATION_NOTES = (
      167      "Daily % change compares the two most recent CLOSED end-of
           -day bars "
      168      "(latest_close vs. previous_close); it never uses a still-
           in-progress session's "

⏺ Everything is clean. Summary of this redesign pass:

  Root-caused and fixed the "15 vs 20" bug: it was a hardcoded string in the
  page header, not a data/filtering bug — plus a real related staleness bug
  found along the way (/api/companies and /api/market-data/portfolio were
  reading the static seed list instead of the live PortfolioRepository), now
  fixed.

  Built: a working light/dark theme system (toggle, persistence, no-flash
  script) on top of CSS tokens that already existed but had no control; a fully
  redesigned Portfolio page (search, sector grouping, explainable status
  categories driven by real event/priority data — never price-movement-alone,
  mobile cards with sparklines from a new small backend history endpoint); a
  restructured Research page (FINN's view → reported facts → collapsed evidence
  → clickable next-questions, with the "no LLM" notice demoted from headline to
  footer); a relabeled Alerts page (real category+priority instead of "Material
  filing", trigger-reason numbers, needs-attention/other sections, watch-rules
  summary); and Home sections (positive developments, market watch, collapsed
  routine items, honest empty state).

  One explicit, documented scope decision: I did not fabricate a qualitative
  "Positive/Mixed/Cautious" investment verdict in Research, since the backend
  has no multi-metric trend-synthesis for ad-hoc Pull questions (only for
  curated Push events) and no LLM is configured — doing so would mean guessing.
  This is called out directly in the report rather than silently cut.

  Verified: backend 523/523 tests passing (5 new), frontend tsc/eslint/next
  build all clean, and every new/changed API checked against real live data from
  this session's own discovered documents/announcements. Screenshots could not
  be captured (sandboxed tool denial, same as a prior phase) — flagged honestly
  as a weaker verification than visual inspection.

❯ # FINN — Fix Daily Intelligence: Build a Real Portfolio Activity Feed

  The current Daily Intelligence page is not fulfilling its purpose.

  The screenshot shows a mostly empty page with:

  - By event / By announcement filters.
  - 24h, 7 days, 30 days and Custom date filters.
  - Announcement category filters.
  - An empty state saying that no announcements have been imported.
  - An explanation that FINN does not currently scrape NSE/BSE live.

  This is not a good first experience for an investor.

  Daily Intelligence is the first page users see. It must provide a useful
  picture of what is happening across their portfolio, even when no price or
  volume alert has triggered.

  **The key distinction: Daily Intelligence is a portfolio activity and
  intelligence feed. Alerts is a threshold-triggered notification centre. They
  must not be treated as the same thing.**

  Implement this properly using real available data. Do not manufacture company
  events.

  ---

  ## 1. Audit the existing data first

  Inspect:

  - Daily Intelligence frontend components.
  - Push API endpoints and services.
  - Announcement/event repositories.
  - Imported announcements and their metadata.
  - Company document discovery from Phase 12B.
  - Existing company documents and their publication dates.
  - Market-data history and EOD refresh.
  - Financial facts and extracted results.
  - Portfolio repository.
  - Existing alert-generation logic.
  - Current filters and their default state.

  Determine exactly which data is available and which sources are currently
  working.

  Inspect why the screenshot displays an empty page.

  Specifically verify:

  1. Is the `Insider Trading` category selected by default?
  2. Is the 24-hour window hiding older useful information?
  3. Is the page only querying manually imported announcements?
  4. Are events being filtered out incorrectly?
  5. Is there relevant company document or financial data that the feed
  currently ignores?
  6. Are ordinary company events incorrectly excluded because they did not
  cross an alert threshold?

  Document the actual root cause.

  Do not assume data exists until the backend has been checked.

  ---

  ## 2. Correct the default feed behaviour

  When the investor opens Daily Intelligence:

  - Default to all event categories.
  - Do not preselect Insider Trading or another narrow category.
  - Keep the 24-hour date selector available.
  - Show all relevant event types by default.
  - Make the active filters visually obvious.
  - Provide a visible Clear filters action.
  - Show the number of matchi
  ──── (715 lines hidden) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  ially when safe parallel execution is possible.

  ### 6. Improve loading experience

  While data is being fetched, show lightweight skeletons that match the final
  layout.

  Examples:

  - Portfolio company-card skeletons.
  - Small chart placeholders.
  - Research answer loading state.
  - Daily Intelligence feed skeletons.

  Show essential page structure immediately.

  Load secondary information progressively.

  Avoid blank screens, frozen interfaces and large blocking spinners.

  Show useful error and retry states when a request fails.

  Do not display fake numbers or placeholder financial charts that could be
  mistaken for real information.

  ### 7. Performance targets

  Measure actual performance before and after optimisation.

  Use these as targets, not fabricated claims:

  - Initial page structure visible within approximately 1–2 seconds on a
  reasonable connection.
  - Cached route navigation should feel nearly immediate.
  - Important portfolio information should appear before secondary charts
  finish loading.
  - Avoid unnecessary blocking requests.
  - Keep animated portfolio graphs smooth on mobile.
  - Avoid noticeable interface freezing during navigation.
  - Keep memory and CPU usage reasonable for the intended free hosting
  configuration.

  Use browser performance tools and API timing logs where available.

  Record the actual results and testing conditions.

  ### 8. Preserve functionality

  Performance improvements must not:

  - Remove financial information.
  - Reduce data accuracy.
  - Break source citations.
  - Hide failed API requests.
  - Disable important background updates.
  - Introduce incorrect cache freshness.
  - Break the light/dark themes.
  - Break mobile graph animations.
  - Weaken authentication or data isolation.

  ### 9. Final performance verification

  Test:

  - First application load.
  - Home page load.
  - Portfolio page load with all 20 companies.
  - Portfolio search and filtering.
  - Animated chart rendering.
  - Research submission and response.
  - Alerts loading.
  - Light/dark theme switching.
  - Navigation between all four main pages.
  - Behaviour on mobile-width screens.
  - Behaviour with a slower network.
  - Behaviour when an API request fails.

  Create `docs/FINN_PERFORMANCE_REPORT.md` documenting:

  - Original bottlenecks.
  - Changes made.
  - Before-and-after timings.
  - Remaining slow operations.
  - Any trade-offs.
  - Actual results.

  **Final principle: Show useful information quickly, load additional detail
  progressively, and never sacrifice financial correctness for perceived
  speed.**

⏺ Done. Summary:

  Daily Intelligence root cause found and fixed: not a category bug, not a
  scraping-coverage bug — the 24h default window genuinely had 0 events (7-day
  window had 64), confirmed live. Added an intelligent fallback (separate
  "latest available activity" section, never silently widening the primary
  filter) plus two new real data streams: Market Pulse (new GET
  /api/market-data/portfolio/pulse endpoint — fixed editorial thresholds,
  independent of user alert config, confirmed live: 10/20 companies crossing
  today) and Fundamental Watch (reuses existing financial_changes, honestly
  shows "no data yet" since financial-metrics import hasn't been done for real
  portfolio companies — zero fabrication).

  Theme/text/animation: trimmed marketing-style subtitles off all 4 pages; added
  draw-on-load sparkline animation (stroke-dashoffset, respects
  prefers-reduced-motion, no re-animation on unrelated re-renders).

  Performance: parallelized the 20 sequential yfinance calls in
  /api/market-data/portfolio (5.1s → 2.7–3.8s, measured, not estimated). Found
  but deliberately did not touch a riskier N+1-style issue in Research's lexical
  search — documented as the top next step rather than rushed.

  A real test-pollution bug was caught and fixed along the way: the new pulse
  endpoint's unmocked PortfolioRepository call leaked a live company_id into a
  shared cache, breaking an unrelated cross-company isolation test — reproduced
  and fixed.

  Backend: 531/531 passing (8 new). Frontend: tsc/eslint/next build clean. Four
  docs written: FINN_DAILY_INTELLIGENCE_UPGRADE.md, FINN_PERFORMANCE_REPORT.md,
  plus the company-timeline view was explicitly deferred (not built) given time
  constraints.

❯ # FINN — Final Project Documentation for ENAM AI Engineer Build Challenge

  Act as a professional technical writer and product documentation specialist.

  Create a polished, concise, approximately **3-page project documentation
  report** for FINN (Financial Investment Agent), developed for the ENAM AI
  Engineer Build Challenge.

  This document will be submitted to ENAM evaluators. It must communicate the
  actual engineering work, product thinking, technical decisions, creativity
  and limitations of the project.

  It should be factual, professional and readable.

  **Avoid marketing language, exaggerated claims, generic AI descriptions and
  unnecessary technical detail. Every paragraph must communicate something
  meaningful.**

  ## 1. Research the actual project first

  Before writing, inspect the repository and read the relevant documentation:

  - Original ENAM Build Challenge brief and financial glossary.
  - README and project architecture.
  - Product gap analysis.
  - Competitive benchmark.
  - Implementation roadmap.
  - Automation foundation.
  - Phase 11 completion report.
  - Phase 12A report.
  - Phase 12B report.
  - Latest Push, Pull and UI implementation reports.
  - Deployment report, if available.
  - Existing API routes, frontend pages, backend services and database schema.

  Inspect the actual implementation wherever necessary.

  Do not rely solely on old reports or previous AI-generated descriptions.

  Verify what is implemented, what has been tested and what is actually
  deployed.

  Do not claim a feature is working merely because a component or API exists.

  ## 2. Document structure

  Use the following structure. Keep the entire report to approximately three
  pages.

  ### PAGE 1 — Product vision and what we built

  **Title: FINN — Financial Investment Agent**

  Include a short introduction explaining the problem FINN addresses:

  Investors receive large amounts of market information, company filings and
  financial data. FINN aims to filter this information and surface the
  developments that deserve attention.

  Explain the two main workflows:

  **Push — Proactive Intelligence**
  FINN monitors available portfolio information, identifies relevant
  developments, prioritises them and presents them through a daily intelligence
  feed and alerts.

  **Pull — On-demand Research**
  Users ask questions about companies and receive research grounded in their
  primary financial documents, with supporting evidence and source references.

  Explain the intended users and the product philosophy: reduce manual rese
  ──── (148 lines hidden) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  explaining the product and the decisions behind it, not as a company
  advertising a product.

  Use first-person plural sparingly where appropriate.

  ## 4. Visual and document formatting

  Produce a clean, professional document.

  Design direction:

  - A4 portrait.
  - Three pages maximum.
  - Modern, restrained visual identity.
  - Deep forest green as the primary accent.
  - Black or charcoal text.
  - White or warm off-white background.
  - Consistent headings and spacing.
  - Readable typography.
  - Small, tasteful tables and diagrams.
  - No unnecessary illustrations or decorative elements.

  Use a professional font such as Aptos, Arial or Inter, with approximately
  10–11 pt body text.

  Use a clear title, section headings, page numbers and consistent margins.

  A small FINN wordmark or simple text heading is sufficient. Do not invent a
  company logo.

  Include a small architecture diagram if it improves clarity and fits within
  the page limit.

  ## 5. Accuracy requirements

  These are mandatory:

  1. Do not invent features, results, tests or data coverage.
  2. Verify the final test count from actual test reports or current test
  execution where feasible.
  3. Do not claim that production deployment is complete unless a public
  deployment has been verified.
  4. Distinguish EOD data from real-time data.
  5. Report document coverage honestly.
  6. Distinguish automatic discovery from successful download and ingestion.
  7. Distinguish deterministic extraction from AI-generated reasoning.
  8. Preserve the correct 20-company portfolio and five ad-hoc companies.
  9. Do not disclose passwords, API keys, tokens or private configuration.
  10. When a fact cannot be verified, omit it or explicitly describe it as a
  limitation.

  Do not spend time making the report longer to include every implementation
  detail. Select the most important decisions and engineering accomplishments.

  ## 6. Deliverables

  Create these files:

  1. `docs/FINN_PROJECT_DOCUMENTATION.md` — editable source.
  2. `FINN_Project_Documentation.pdf` — polished submission-ready PDF.
  3. `FINN_Project_Documentation.docx` — editable Word version.

  Keep all three versions consistent.

  Render and inspect the PDF to verify:
  - Exactly three pages or fewer.
  - No clipped text.
  - No broken tables.
  - No awkward page breaks.
  - Readable fonts.
  - Consistent formatting.

  The final result should be a concise but convincing account of what was
  actually built, how it works, what made the engineering challenging and what
  was deliberately left for future development.

❯ Inspect the FINN-ENAM frontend code, especially frontend/lib/api.ts and all
  API configuration files. Tell me:
  1. The exact environment variable name required for the backend API base URL.
  2. Whether it uses a NEXT_PUBLIC_ prefix.
  3. The exact format of the backend URL expected.
  4. Any other environment variables required for Vercel deployment.

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Findings (read-only inspection, no files modified):

  1. Environment variable name: NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL — this is the only one
     referenced anywhere in the frontend code (lib/api.ts:17, the single place
     every API call goes through).
  2. NEXT_PUBLIC_ prefix: Yes. It must have that prefix since it's read
     client-side (in the browser) via process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL —
     Next.js only inlines env vars into client bundles if they're prefixed
     NEXT_PUBLIC_. A plain API_BASE_URL without the prefix would be undefined in
     the browser.
  3. Expected URL format: it should include the /api path suffix, e.g.
     https://your-backend-host.com/api — not just the bare domain. The code does
     `${API_BASE_URL}${path}` where path already starts with / (e.g.
     /companies), so API_BASE_URL itself should not have a trailing slash. Local
     default: http://127.0.0.1:8000/api.
  4. Other env vars for Vercel deployment: none found anywhere in the frontend
     codebase — NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL is the only one referenced. Two things
     worth flagging though (not env vars, but deployment-relevant):
     - This is a hardcoded fallback value (http://127.0.0.1:8000/api) baked into
       the code if the var is unset — on Vercel you must set it explicitly or
       the deployed frontend will try to call localhost from the visitor's
       browser.
     - Separately, on the backend side (not frontend, but required for this to
       work end-to-end): app/core/config.py's cors_allow_origins is currently
       hardcoded to localhost:3000/127.0.0.1:3000 — the deployed Vercel
       frontend's origin would need to be added there (noted in
       docs/FINN_DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md from an earlier phase) or the browser
       will block the requests with a CORS error regardless of the frontend env
       var being set correctly.
